| S7 | `S7-01` Site Health and bounded asset state | Distinguish gateway freshness from battery stress where source-backed; otherwise show the battery claim as Unknown. |

Every packet names **one visible change to a mock surface**, the exact persisted object/read API that makes it real, a runnable demo path, a missing/conflicting-evidence state, and an evidence-lineage check. Tasks may carry migrations, adapters, API and UI together. Do not create isolated â€œPostgres schemaâ€, â€œgateway frameworkâ€, â€œanalytics frameworkâ€ or â€œtab shellâ€ tasks whose only visible result is infrastructure. If a prerequisite needs its own task, its closeout must still change a specific inspectable Site or Lab state and name the next screen it unlocks.

Optimize the critical path for four owner-review moments: **S2 evidence arrives**, **S4 an operational claim has bounded money**, **S6 an intervention has a verified or inconclusive outcome**, and **S7 Site Health completes the mini-grid story**. Use one active slice at a time, small fixtures and focused checks; avoid full estate seed work, extra domains, global indexes and polish until the Site journey works. The target platform contract stays intact, while only robustness required to make the current visible claim credible lands now.

## Simulation and edge workstream

This is a cross-cutting dependency stream alongside the Site screens. It owns source production and delivery, never product claims. The Architect has set S2-01a/b/c as the reviewed cuts and retained S2-02 as the evidence owner checkpoint. From S1-01 until S2-01a closes, the imported Kobo Lab Draft/run/Commit path is deliberately blocked; unimported legacy YAML Sites remain available under their existing boundary. The short S2-01a cut restores registered Kobo Lab runs, while Commit remains blocked until S2-02.

| Gate | Owner and planned cut | Proof before the consuming screen closes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Registered Lab basis | `A-lab-registered-basis`; S2-01a after S1-02 | A Kobo run selects a versioned Postgres Foundation/binding snapshot, records its basis, and emits only public device reports. Draft/run opens on the registered basis; Commit waits for S2-02. Historical Stage stays read-only. S6 policy-change runs reuse this path. |
| Durable simulated gateway | S2-01b | Separate process, SQLite WAL outbox, persisted sequence and V3 identity, sealed canonical Batch V2 bytes, retries and receipt slots survive restart. Simulated reports use the common adapter interface. S2-02 adds authenticated HTTPS delivery and central dispositions. The S2 demo does not prove a physical protocol parser. |
| Central diagnostics and faults | `A-edge-diagnostics`; local publication/faults in S2-01c, authenticated central storage in S2-02, Site Health consumer in S7 | GatewayDiagnosticsV1 is a separate operational report with registered identity, local sequence and server receive time. Show last contact, queue age/depth, clock offset and disk pressure; demonstrate disconnect/retry and visible acquisition stop on disk pressure. Lab shows local state; Site Health uses centrally received facts. S1 Readiness/SLD remains configuration-only until operational reporting is supported. |
| Batch security choice | `A-edge-batch-signature` decision | Defer per-batch signatures and key rotation for the simulated S2 pilot. Keep registered TLS gateway credentials, canonical SHA-256 digest and exact-byte retry. If a real-pilot security need later selects signing, amend the technical design and Batch V2 contract before implementation. |
| Repeatable mini-grid scenarios | `A-demo-scenarios`; S2-01c faults, S3-01 dispatch, S6-02 verification | Complete/missing/contradictory/late public intervals; two separate runs under old then new effective policy with later guardrail failure and inconclusive evidence. No mid-run Foundation switch is required. S7 battery stress stays Unknown unless the simulator gains a causal public observation; `I-sim-battery-cases` is a follow-on producer. All product fixtures pass normal ingestion. |
| Cold-chain and EV source worlds | Later domain waves, after owner tier and role/analytic decisions | Each domain gets a simulated world/device model, domain device adapter and captured physical-report peer, site-specific roles, missing/disturbed cases and multi-Site windows through the same gateway and ingestion path. These are additional effort gates for the domain waves, not work hidden inside a UI task. |

At the pre-S3 planning review, obtain owner tiers by subject area with screen exceptions and review actor identity, Finding/Action routes and shared projection consumers. S4 and S6 consume those decisions; their technical prerequisites remain on the relevant task nodes. Start V-01 visual comparison after S2 Evidence is reviewable, then compare each activated screen at desktop and narrow widths and trace displayed values to persisted source IDs.

The recorded diagnostics contract is a separate authenticated `GatewayDiagnosticsV1` report rather than a `SourceEnvelope`: registered gateway/site identity, observation time, local sequence, last device contact by source, queue count and oldest age, disk pressure, clock offset and mapping errors. The server records its own receipt time and computes central freshness from that time and a declared cadence; a missing heartbeat is Unknown/stale, never a healthy reading. Reports are idempotent by gateway/sequence and are not accepted telemetry. The [technical design](production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md) now owns the table, endpoint and wire fields; S2-01c/S2-02 packets refine cadence and focused crash tests.

**Physical peer source in effect:** S2 uses simulated reports only. At the first real pilot, choose one real-device capture if available, or one labelled synthetic fixture built from vendor protocol documentation; prove protocol frames, register scaling, units and device clocks before claiming real-adapter equivalence. That future gate replaces the simulator batch while preserving the backend, analytic and UI contracts. It is not an S2 acceptance check. The S7 demo cannot use captured-device replay as a battery fallback under this source choice.

## Shared implementation rules

- **Immediate demo scope versus target design:** S1â€“S2 need crash-safe SQLite enqueue, stable identities, authenticated batch ingestion, durable per-record decisions and Postgres Site/evidence storage. Build the minimal diagnostics needed to inspect delivery. Detailed queue retention policy, elaborate backpressure controls, multi-protocol drivers, broker transport, full bitemporal editing UI and generic streaming infrastructure can follow when pilots require them. Database/wire schemas should leave room without falsely claiming those behaviors are shipped.
- **Typed analytic boundary:** `EvidenceQuery` returns typed normalized readings/records with quality, time, asset/role, configuration basis and accepted-evidence IDs. The dispatch analytic consumes this plus a typed public configuration view. SQL rows, `jsonb`, canonical envelope text, Lab world records and source adapters stop at repository/normalization boundaries. Contract tests compare simulated and captured real inputs after this boundary.
- **Episode identity:** decide continuity tolerance, episode anchor and recurrence linkage in S3/S4 domain design, with a short decision table before coding the first Finding. `Finding.id` names a durable operational episode; `FindingRevision` describes reassessment of that episode. A new separated episode is a new Finding, linked as recurrence if relevant. Do not use a changing model output hash as Finding identity.
- **Domain algorithms:** dispatch thresholds, cold-chain excursion detection and EV session logic are domain-design work. The platform plan requires their inputs, revision/lineage outputs and claim limits, not more universal framework or algorithm prose. S1â€“S7 cover one mini-grid Site; later domains add role sets and analytics through the same seam.
- **Verification gates:** each slice exercises the screen at its selected window, the read API, storage/reload, a missing/contradictory evidence case, and lineage back to an accepted SourceEnvelope. S2 adds loss/retry/duplicate/conflict; S3â€“S4 add late evidence and revision preservation; S6 adds guardrail failure. Tests should assert behavior, not mock labels.

## Handoff beyond Site

After S4, a **Findings index** can already list real Finding objects, but build the global root only when the Site detail and lifecycle are useful. After S6, **Actions** and **Sites** become filtering/indexing over durable Site/Action summaries; **Portfolio** aggregates comparable consequences and priorities with currency/window/coverage boundaries; **Evidence** aggregates Site capability/readiness; **Assets** indexes Site Asset/Health/work records after S7. These are later product roots, not prerequisites or empty screens for the Site spine. Cold-chain and EV are later Site-domain passes using the same ingestion, revision, work and shell contracts; neither should be built by copying mini-grid screens with different labels.

**Planner output requested next:** perform the archive and routing update, publish the new S1â€“S7 queue, and prepare S1 packets with mock-screen state, API/read-model contract, migration seam, acceptance fixture, reviewer gate and explicit deferments. Do not activate or implement an old T0xx task merely because its title resembles a new result.

## plan/S5-01-M3
"""S4-02b phase 2b: label the authored demonstration Finding in product reads.

Owner ruling `.agent/owner-S4-02b-money-demo-2026-10-14.md`: the `P01-power` case of
`conversions/s4-power-demo-cases-v1.json` is authored demonstration content and must be visibly labelled
as such beside ordinary Findings. Nothing in the evidence rows records that authorship (the case is posted
with the registered gateway credential, like every S4 case), so the label is derived from the spec's own
declaration: its Site code and its case days. The spec's days are tested clear of every S4 case day.

This is a presentation label only. It never changes a quantity, price, amount or currentness.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from datetime import date, datetime, timedelta, timezone
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

SPEC_PATH = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "conversions" / "s4-power-demo-cases-v1.json"

LABEL = "Demonstration data"
STATEMENT = ("This finding comes from demonstration readings written by the AssetOps team to show a complete "
             "fuel-cost calculation. They are not measurements from a working generator.")


UNVERIFIED_LABEL = "Source not confirmed"
UNVERIFIED_STATEMENT = ("Whether this finding comes from demonstration readings could not be checked, so treat any "
                        "cost shown here with caution.")


class SpecUnreadable(Exception):
    pass


@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
def _declared() -> tuple[str, str, tuple[tuple[str, date], ...]]:
    # Fails closed: an unreadable or malformed spec raises (and is therefore never cached), and the caller
    # then labels the Finding cautiously rather than leaving possible demonstration money unlabelled.
    try:
        spec = json.loads(SPEC_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        return (spec["site_code"], spec["label"],
                tuple((c["case_id"], date.fromisoformat(c["day"])) for c in spec["cases"]))
    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as exc:
        raise SpecUnreadable(str(exc)) from exc


def _instant(value: Any) -> datetime | None:
    if value is None:
        return None
    if isinstance(value, datetime):
        return value if value.tzinfo else value.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)
    try:
        parsed = datetime.fromisoformat(str(value).replace("Z", "+00:00"))
    except ValueError:
        return None
    return parsed if parsed.tzinfo else parsed.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)


def demonstration_case(site_code: str, start: Any, end: Any) -> dict[str, str] | None:
    """The declared demonstration case whose UTC day holds `[start, end)` at this Site, else None.

    Fails closed: an unreadable spec gives a cautious "source not confirmed" label, and an open episode
    (no end) is judged by its start day, so demonstration money is never shown unlabelled.
    """
    try:
        code, spec_label, cases = _declared()
    except SpecUnreadable:
        return {"label": UNVERIFIED_LABEL, "statement": UNVERIFIED_STATEMENT, "case_id": "unverified", "spec": ""}
    lo, hi = _instant(start), _instant(end)
    if site_code != code or lo is None:
        return None
    for case_id, day in cases:
        day_start = datetime(day.year, day.month, day.day, tzinfo=timezone.utc)
        if day_start <= lo and (hi is None or hi <= day_start + timedelta(days=1)) and lo < day_start + timedelta(days=1):
            return {"label": LABEL, "statement": STATEMENT, "case_id": case_id, "spec": spec_label}
    return None

.agent\architect-S4-05-followup-log.txt:589:+The blanket `UNKNOWN` default is intentional for unbound historical rows and genuinely unknown or broken input, but not for every future ordinary simulated run. Separate source class from authorship. S4-05 must support two positive, immutable, server-authorized pre-admission batch bindings: `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` for an identified authored fixture/spec version (P01 and the authored P02 late-power fixture), and `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` for ordinary simulator output with a server-recorded run ID, immutable run identity/digest and exact tenant/Site, gateway/source, batch ID and payload digest. The latter requires a registered `SIMULATED` gateway and proof that this exact batch was produced by that recorded run; `source_mode=SIMULATED`, a gateway credential, client text, Site/day match or a private manifest alone does not grant the designation. The server may keep private scenario truth outside product evidence; the public binding carries only run identity and provenance needed for the read. Extend the same ledger publication/admission lock, append-only binding, duplicate and no-retroactive-backfill rules to this class. Do not reinterpret `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` as authored fixture or physical measurement.

.agent\planner-S4-05-fix-log.txt:1763:-4. **Read and amount rules (new).** Resolve every cited accepted ID under the requested tenant/Site, returning publication ID, designation and fixture/spec version per record plus a set-level state: `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` only when every relevant cited ID is bound to a consistent authored publication basis; `MIXED` when some cited IDs have that designation and others have no designation or a different basis; `UNKNOWN` when none is designated or any cited ID/link cannot be resolved (and retain exact problematic IDs). .agent\planner-S4-05-fix-log.txt:3418:.ai/DECISIONS.md:4145:Decision: `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`. Under the owner's 2026-10-15 absence delegation, accept S4-05 Q1-Q3 as amended by Follow-up (plan review M1) in `.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md`. A server-authorized, immutable pre-admission publication binds an exact Batch V2 commitment to tenant/Site, gateway/source, external batch ID and canonical digest. `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` additionally binds fixture/spec ID and version, including authored P01 and P02. `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` binds an ordinary simulator batch to a server-recorded run identity; `SIMULATED` mode or a private manifest alone is insufficient. Publication and admission serialize on the scoped batch key; post-admission registration and mismatched commitments fail. Accepted IDs inherit immutable provenance, duplicates retain their original accepted ID, and historical unbound IDs stay unknown. Finding and consequence source state derives from each revision's exact accepted IDs, including consequence power intervals. Coherent all-authored or all-registered-simulation sets may show a current amount only when LR eligibility, exact basis, currentness, source-open and quantity/price gates also pass. Mixed or unknown source blocks a current amount in Finding Financials, consequence and S5 Overview regardless of `CURRENT PRICED` or LR eligibility; prior values remain audit-only. Real data remains unknown and unable to show current money until a separate server-authorized `ATTESTED_REAL_SOURCE` follow-on is implemented. Create open registry node `I-attested-real-source-provenance` in `real_pilot_requires` before resolving `I-stored-demo-provenance` or installing a pilot timer; S4-05 may resolve before the real-source node, but its resolution is not pilot readiness. Amend S5-01's source gate to consume stored exact-revision provenance whichever slice merges second. Extend the existing PostgreSQL evidence-ledger writer, not the writer count: rule 8 permits only its INSERTs to publication/link rows, refuses other writers and mutations, preserves existing writer protections, and is backed by database immutability. Visible copy and pilot designation still require the owner's W1 checkpoint; this decision grants neither owner approval nor W1/pilot readiness.

**W1 task order â€” recommend option (b).** After S2-02 closes, scoring may reorder S3â€“S7 implementation when the tasks are technically independent and the owner checkpoints still occur. The present queue and registry checker encode a linear `sequenced_after` chain, and some outline `Depends on` lines repeat it; those are currently binding until the Planner explicitly reconciles the queue, packets and checker under prerequisite 1/3. Mark each predecessor as either a real technical `requires` edge or an ordering-only edge; remove/rewrite only the latter before ranking can select around it. Keep technical `requires`, fixture and review gates even if their score is lower. This is preferable to option (a), which would make W1 scoring informational despite independent SourceHealth work and would delay the owner's requested early ranking. No task is activated on a proposed reorder while the executable queue still says otherwise.

**Shared-contract priority.** FindingIndex, CapabilityReadiness, SourceHealth, money comparability, routes and actor identity named as pre-S3 gates are mandatory Architect decisions, **outside competitive ranking**; their cross-domain value does not depend on W2/W3 screens being approved yet. Optional shared contracts are scored only against approved active-wave consumers, so the score may understate later-wave benefit. The Architect flags that limitation at each wave start and asks the owner to promote a genuinely necessary shared contract to an explicit gate; no speculative later-wave benefit is silently added to `D`, `B` or `R`.

**XL escalation.** Add `XL` as a planning estimate for work larger than one reviewable L slice; it has no autonomous score. Split an XL candidate into S/M/L reviewable slices or ask the owner to choose a bounded exception. An L candidate may proceed normally. This replaces the unreachable `>L` comparison.

**Autonomy and stops.** After wave-start owner input and S2-02 closeout, the coordinator may close out accepted work, recalculate, select an eligible top candidate, refine, build, obtain independent review, and merge under `.ai/WORKFLOW.md`. Only the coordinator records review outcomes and owner approvals after reading the underlying evidence; no Implementer commissions its own review. A `USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED` checkpoint blocks that task's acceptance/merge until owner review is recorded. Stop for owner at wave start (IDs, core panels, demo values and K), panel withholding, S2/S4/S6/S7 review checkpoints and each wave exit, screen dropping, a **â‰¤5%** contested ranking, XL escalation, or scoring-parameter change. Also stop on checker failure, a second review rejection of the same task, or any real claim lacking lineage. Let `gap=(top scoreâˆ’second score)/max(abs(top score),1)` for the two eligible candidates. At **gapâ‰¤5%**, stop and present the scores, raw facts and recommended tie-break choice to the owner. At **5%<gapâ‰¤10%**, apply the published order of fewer unresolved technical gates, lower effort, then stable node ID to choose between those two, notify the owner with both scores and reasons (the notice is recorded in `.agent/coordinator-handoff.md`, so the owner sees it on resuming a session), and continue without awaiting a reply. At **gap>10%**, select the highest score without a ranking notification; with only one eligible candidate, select it without a ranking notification. Other owner gates stop regardless of gap. No silent override of a stopped gate.

**V-01 adoption gate.** Recommend that, at S2-02 independent review, S2 owner Evidence closeout and pre-S3 refinement, the owner **adopt the refined [V-01 proposal](../tasks/V-01-v69-visual-fidelity.md) as a W1 item**, with exact supported manifest IDs, core panels, mock comparison method and acceptance boundary. The Planner then makes V-01 executable; its app screenshots and checks cover only supported real data and honest missing states. Standing side-by-side comparisons remain part of each slice, but **no screen is fully `Real` until the applicable V-01 visual/interaction acceptance is actually recorded**. If adoption is withheld, keep V-01 non-executable and full `Real` blocked; do not infer approval from this strategy.

**Current review routing.** Preserve the owner's specific S2 instruction: fresh Claude independent review per slice, one Codex review of the S2-01c/S2-02 whole, and the S2 Evidence owner checkpoint. Do not infer a new Codex review per slice from this autonomy loop. **Product-node recording.** Once W1 is confirmed, record its approved nodes as `Real with honest gaps`, `priority: W1`, with exact approved core panels, owner evidence and demo values. Other nodes remain `pending owner` with a non-authoritative planned-wave field until their wave starts. Do not pre-mark all current W1 nodes real merely because S1 is merged. A node's status describes its approved target, not completion of every panel; panel evidence and `to_real` gates remain separate.

Decision: `D-2026-10-09-s2-rotation-reconciliation-followup`. Codex Architect, resolving S2-02 Q37â€“Q40 and fix re-review R3 in `.agent/architect-pre-S3-answers.md` after S2 closeout; this is a follow-up contract, not a reopened S2 verdict. For the same-account pilot, `ISSUE_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` may be a supervised operator halt without a local marker; a later successful issue on a readable DB creates a new protected file and revokes earlier selectors atomically. A marker alone is not a trusted second authority. The current fresh *unlocked* no-selector read is **not conclusive proof of rollback**: the original transaction may still finish its COMMIT. Before any non-demo/unattended rotation or real pilot, a bounded S2-02r correction must reconcile under the same gateway-row `FOR UPDATE` lock used by issuance, wait for any prior transaction to finish, then check selector/verifier, credential reference, expected source enablement and survival of the previously active credential in that locked transaction. Only proven non-commit with the old credential active permits deleting the new file; timeout or inconsistent state keeps the protected file and typed unknown outcome. `pg_stat_activity` alone does not establish this ordering. Distinguish a provably pre-transaction failure from an ambiguous COMMIT in that follow-up; the present conservative unknown result is a carry, not secret loss. The v3 store-root-bound comparison correctly closes on older servers until restart; its hash of canonical resolved Evidence/commit roots is accepted, and a moved store needs a fresh report. S2-02r is not an S3 analytic prerequisite.

Decision: `D-2026-10-09-pre-s3-claim-routes-and-actor`. Codex Architect, resolving bounded `A-claim-work-routes` and `A-actor-identity` before S3 activation under the owner-confirmed delivery strategy; detail in `.agent/architect-pre-S3-answers.md`. A Finding or Action has stable UUID identity under tenant and Site UUID across mini-grid, cold-chain and EV. Canonical Site-scoped routes are `/sites/{site_code}/findings/{finding_id}` and `/sites/{site_code}/actions/{action_id}`, with parallel `/api/sites/{site_code}/...` reads. Resolve Site code within the authenticated tenant; a global future index links to these same IDs. Finding history selects an immutable `finding_revision_id` (optional `revision_id` query) at stated `knowledge_at`; omitted revision selects latest permitted and returns its selected ID/watermark. Action state derives from ActionEvents and selects an exact event/cutoff, while plan/outcome child views select their own immutable IDs; do not invent an ActionRevision. Back-links preserve Site, window and exact claim selection. Unauthenticated is 401; unauthorized known scope is 403; foreign-tenant or nonexistent identity and foreign revision are 404. A supported but unbuilt/lagging read reports typed unavailable or pending, not a fabricated empty claim; unbuilt tabs stay hidden or honestly unavailable.

Every auditable assumption, policy or work write stores server-resolved `actor_kind`, `actor_id`, source channel, recorded time and authorization basis. A human `author_id` requires an authenticated human principal; client text, local OS username, fixed development tenant and gateway credential are not human identity. A `SYSTEM` action has stable service-principal and triggering job/command/fixture provenance, without implying a human attestation. Human attestation binds exact content/version, time and immutable proof; correction appends a new event/revision. S4-02 price edits and S6-01 Action/policy writes require a verified operator path or explicitly labelled server-authorized system demo operation; otherwise writes are unavailable. The S2 provisioning CLI's OS-user value remains local context. These bounded decisions are mandatory before S3-01 planning activation but are implementation gates of S4/S6, not dispatch inputs.

Decision: `D-2026-10-11-s3-dispatch-interval-assessment`. Codex Architect resolves bounded `A-S3-dispatch` for mini-grid S3-01; Planner refinement inputs are in `.agent/architect-S3-dispatch-answers.md`. This decision uses the typed seam and Postgres revision rules in `frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md` Â§Â§3â€“4 and the existing `backend/tests/dispatch_decision_table.md` (`dispatch-2`) as the behavioral comparison baseline. It authorizes no file-backed product read after S3 cutover and no cold-chain/EV analytic implementation.

**Input and cohort.** `EvidenceQuery` is a tenant-authorized, Site/domain-scoped query for a UTC half-open window at `knowledge_at`; it returns typed normalized accepted readings, original interval/source times, quality, canonical role/asset/unit, exact accepted evidence IDs and content digests, as-accepted Foundation/binding revisions and source/sequence-epoch provenance. It selects by source-time or reported-interval overlap, including bracketing evidence needed to bound continuity, with the chosen knowledge cutoff applied to server acceptance/recording. `AnalyticInputSet` freezes the selected public evidence cohort, role coverage/missingness, effective public configuration, analytic/rule version, window and cutoff; its canonical SHA-256 covers sorted evidence IDs **and digests**, cohort identity, relevant Foundation/binding revision IDs and values, role coverage, window and model version. SQL rows, raw envelope JSON, gateway diagnostics, Lab world/truth, private run IDs and file-backed assessment objects stop before this boundary. `RUN_VIRTUAL` source time is a simulation clock, not real-world contact freshness.

Overlapping accepted simulated reports are separate evidence, not one physical series. The selected basis is an explicit, persisted **public report cohort**: for each required source, registered source UUID and sequence epoch, with the included batch/evidence IDs and common Site/configuration/window basis; the request/read returns that selection. A multi-source cohort must be proven coherent by its public gateway/source-epoch and sealed batch provenance. The private Lab run manifest may guide a demo operator to those public IDs but is not an analytic input or authority. S2's two overlapping runs and labelled `fault-fixture-epoch` must remain separate choices. If there is no unique/provably coherent cohort, return a limited/Indeterminate result naming the ambiguity; never prefer the latest receipt, sum epochs, use the fault fixture as ordinary output or deduplicate distinct evidence by timestamp. Within a selected cohort, same-role/time disagreements remain conflicts rather than arbitrarily choosing a value.

**Dispatch rule.** The first Postgres model is a **new version** of the mini-grid dispatch rule; it may reuse the published `dispatch-2` thresholds/decision table but must name every carried or changed rule in a versioned table and compare its results. Reconstruct generator ON/OFF intervals with observed bounds/gaps. For each segment, apply evidenced constraints in order: an authorized, supported override or effective minimum runtime; battery reserve/near-reserve; PV-plus-battery capability shortfall; then candidate headroom. A whole interval is Necessary only if every assessed segment is Necessary; any supported Candidate segment gives `CANDIDATE_AVOIDABLE` only for those bounded segments; otherwise it is Indeterminate. Missing role, poor quality, contradiction, open bound or ambiguity names the affected segment/limit. Candidate requires accepted **available PV power** and PCS **discharge capability** (the published `dispatch-2` PV-alone branch also requires evidenced PCS availability), plus the load and, for battery-supported headroom, SOC and selected Foundation reserve basis; observed `pv_power` and rated/actual `battery_power` are not substitutes. The optional available-power and discharge-capability roles require a separately published, versioned binding vocabulary and actual registered readings before S3 can make a candidate claim; `minigrid-roles-1` remains historically interpretable. The S1 Foundation's time-valid `battery_reserve` control is authoritative; S2's measurement-only V3 path supplies no accepted POLICY/OPERATIONAL_RECORD override. Do not fabricate one from a Foundation change or Lab event. Missing optional override records do not by themselves block a Candidate, but the result must say no command record was observed, not that no command occurred; any conflicting supported constraint makes the affected portion Indeterminate under the versioned rule. A supported policy/command record later needs its own versioned provenance and conflict contract before it can change this rule. Necessary policy-only claims require that supported record; otherwise classify from evidenced physical constraints or Indeterminate. Dispatch states a technical candidate, not recovered fuel/cost, physical equipment condition, causal intent or a Finding; S4 owns Finding identity and money.

**Revisions and jobs.** S3 consumes the S2 `dispatch` recompute queue transactionally with a lease/retry, expands an accepted record's affected source-time interval to continuity neighbors, and reruns at a recorded cutoff. Backdated Foundation/binding publication and analytic-model changes enqueue affected windows too. The worker persists an idempotent `AnalyticRun` keyed by Site, analytic/model version, window and input hash, its exact evidence-ID set, and append-only `AssessmentRevision` rows under a stable interval/asset assessment key. Identical input and output produce no new revision; a changed evidence/configuration/model basis appends a new revision while preserving the earlier one and its selected read URL. Projection replacement and job completion occur atomically only for the still-current input/watermark; a newly dirtied overlapping window must remain dirty for another pass, never be lost by a leased worker. Late accepted evidence may change Necessary/Candidate/Indeterminate but never rewrites an earlier revision. The Performance list/detail returns selected revision, rule version, input hash, coverage, evidence IDs, source and analytic watermarks, built time and explicit limitations.

**Projection status and cutover.** `NO_PENDING_DISPATCH` in the S2 Evidence read means only that no S2 job is outstanding; it is never a current assessment. Once S3 owns Performance, use `UNASSESSED` when no completed assessment exists, `PENDING` for a first run in progress, `CURRENT` only when a completed projection's input/source watermark matches accepted evidence and no overlapping dirty/leased/failed job remains, `STALE_PROJECTION` when a previous result exists but any such job or watermark mismatch remains, and `FAILED` when computation failed without a usable current result. Preserve prior revision links under stale/failed status and show job/error reason; no state silently means healthy, Necessary or zero. Reconcile the Evidence projection display to these truthful states after S3 activation. Before switching Performance reads to Postgres, compare representative file-backed dispatch claims against the same public evidence/configuration basis and the new model, recording matched outcomes, bounded intentional rule differences, lineage and exceptions; no private truth or stale JSON becomes product authority. If comparable baseline evidence is unavailable, record the exception and keep the affected read cutover closed rather than claim parity.

**Fixture and reuse boundary.** `I-sim-dispatch-cases` supplies a versioned, repeatable manifest of complete, missing-role/quality, contradictory same-role/time and late/out-of-order public intervals, with expected classification ceiling, selected public cohort, source/configuration revisions and exact batch/evidence IDs. Include the S2 overlapping runs and fault epoch as an ambiguity/exclusion check; prove at least one supported Candidate only after the available-PV/PCS-capability binding and accepted-report gate closes, plus Necessary and Indeterminate cases. Seal and upload every product fixture via the registered simulated gateway, Batch V2 and ordinary authenticated S2 ingestion; rejected siblings remain rejected and private oracle data stays Lab-only. The shared `EvidenceQuery`/`AnalyticInputSet`, run/revision, watermark and claim-limit contracts can be reused by later cold-chain/EV analytics, whose role sets, domain rules, fixtures and UI remain later-wave work.

A separate demo-manifest annotation may identify its fixture origin, explicitly attributed to that manifest; neither the analytic nor the product infers a trusted fixture flag from the epoch name. The read must never silently present a known fixture as ordinary positive output. A trusted central fixture marker is a later S2 acquisition-contract change, not an S3-01 schema shortcut.

Phase 3 may derive `UNASSESSED | PENDING | CURRENT | STALE_PROJECTION | FAILED` from immutable runs/revisions and S2 jobs instead of adding `site_window_projection`, provided a consistent scoped read proves the current accepted input basis and overlapping job state and does not select by `completed_at` alone. If it cannot prove freshness and atomic selection, it must add an atomic projection before cutover. The phase-2 worker's clock-based `knowledge_cutoff` is not a commit-ordered acceptance fence: an in-flight transaction may later commit a disposition timestamped before K. Therefore a selected historical revision is reproducible by its stored run/evidence IDs and digest, but arbitrary replay at K is not; phase 3 must not offer or describe arbitrary `knowledge_at` time travel as reproducible until a commit-ordered ledger watermark is implemented and reviewed as an S2 acquisition follow-up. Later acceptance still creates a dirty job and cannot leave a stale run marked CURRENT. No further owner product decision is requested; the S3-01 owner checkpoint remains.

Decision: `D-2026-10-11-s3-phase-3a-version-and-cutover`. Codex Architect resolves S3-01 phase-3a Q9â€“Q13 in `.agent/architect-S3-answers-3.md`, subject to its independent review. The stricter default cohort rule (two epochs for one source in a UTC window are ambiguous even when their spans do not overlap) and the named interval limitations lifted to the window result are material persisted assessment semantics. Name the corrected model `dispatch-3.1` before phase-3b/owner exposure, update its decision table and consumers, and automatically reconcile/reassess every stored default `dispatch-3` window under the new version. Preserve all `dispatch-3` runs/revisions/URLs unchanged as historical results; they may be shown stale or pinned, never selected as CURRENT under `dispatch-3.1`. A version change invalidates a prior comparison report, so rerun the current-environment gate after reassessment.

Performance cutover stays closed on every `MISSING_SOURCE` exception, `UNPROVEN` comparison, lineage break, stale model or changed baseline; a documented exception is not parity. An explicit empty legacy-claim set may pass only as a scoped zero-inventory statement, not proof that claim classifications match, with positive accepted-evidence/UI paths still reviewed before owner acceptance. `INTENTIONAL_DELTA` requires a claim-specific, versioned `dispatch-2` to `dispatch-3.1` rule explanation and verified comparable accepted public basis; merely finding any `INDETERMINATE` interval and any named window limitation does not prove causation and is `UNPROVEN` until demonstrated. With a matching current basis but an overlapping PENDING/LEASED job, the read remains `STALE_PROJECTION` (`RECOMPUTE_OUTSTANDING`), while `PENDING` is for no selected run. Phase-3b UI must show selected model/revision, ambiguity or limitation, stale/job reason, empty-set caveat and comparison/gate state without implying fuel or money. No new owner product choice is requested; the S3-01 owner walkthrough is still required.

Decision: `D-2026-10-12-finding-episode-and-revisions`. Codex Architect resolves `A-finding-episode` after the owner's contested-ranking choice of option A in `.agent/architect-A-finding-episode-request.md`; detailed S4-01 refinement and S3-01 Q14â€“Q22 rulings are in `.agent/architect-A-finding-episode-answers.md`. The first S4-01 mini-grid Finding is a **candidate generator-support episode**, not every Indeterminate assessment. Create it only from a CURRENT, coherently selected `dispatch-3.1` (or later reviewed dispatch-3.x) interval with a bounded `CANDIDATE_AVOIDABLE` segment and accepted public evidence. Indeterminate alone creates no avoidability Finding; if it replaces evidence for an existing episode, append a limited/unconfirmed revision without asserting that the issue disappeared or was fixed. Necessary-only or unassessed windows create no candidate Finding. A distinct evidence-quality Finding type would need its own later rule and fixture.

**Identity and continuity.** Persist one tenant/Site/domain/asset/mechanism-scoped Finding UUID and immutable `finding_key` when the first supported candidate span is recorded. The key is allocated from the persisted episode identity/anchor, not recomputed from a mutable first-ON timestamp, assessment key, model output hash, UTC day or private Lab run. Match later selected candidate spans to that episode across day boundaries when they overlap or are separated by at most the declared 20-minute dispatch continuity tolerance and there is no contradictory/ambiguous basis across the gap. An evidenced separation greater than 20 minutes creates a new Finding linked by `recurs_after_finding_id`; it is not revision 2 of the first. Persist the match rule version, observed bounds, anchor, cohort and selected window(s). A late earlier report can extend the episode without renaming it. If new evidence splits a previously single episode, retain the original ID for the portion containing its immutable anchor, create new linked Finding(s) for other supported portions, and append a revision explaining the changed coverage. If a later bridge joins two already persisted episodes, keep both old URLs/history, choose the earlier stable anchor (then UUID) as canonical, and record an explicit superseded/merged link on the other; never silently collapse or reuse IDs.

**Revisions and lifecycle.** Each changed selected assessment basis, supported candidate span, limitation or rule version appends an immutable FindingRevision under the same episode only when its canonical Finding basis changes; retry of the same basis is idempotent. A revision cites the exact selected interval AssessmentRevision and its AnalyticRun/model version, plus the selected window AssessmentRevision and every additional interval revision used for a cross-window episode, with exact accepted evidence IDs/digests and time-valid Foundation/binding provenance. Older `dispatch-3` results may be pinned as history but cannot create or refresh a CURRENT Finding under `dispatch-3.1`; later rules create new FindingRevisions, never relabel old ones. A CURRENT complete assessment proving the candidate span no longer supported appends a `NO_LONGER_SUPPORTED` revision and closes the active candidate claim, without asserting operational repair or savings. A stale, failed, ambiguous or Indeterminate assessment instead leaves the prior revision inspectable but marks current support unconfirmed; it does not auto-close. Reappearance after a proven separated interval creates a recurrence Finding and link. Model the close/reopen/split/merge transition as durable recorded history, not an overwritten claim.

**Read boundary.** S4-01 persists Finding/immutable FindingRevision, matching/lifecycle links, and a Site-scoped selected detail read/UI at `/sites/{site_code}/findings/{finding_id}` with optional exact `revision_id`, authenticated tenant/Site scope, selected dispatch revision/model/window/cohort, source/analytic watermark and exact lineage. The selected revision remains addressable after late evidence. Distinguish `NOT_ASSESSED`/projection lag or failed recompute, assessed `NO_FINDING`, `CURRENT` supported Finding, `UNCONFIRMED` prior Finding and closed/recurring history; a zero-row response is not proof of no issue without a current assessed basis. S4-03 owns the full Evidence/History child views, S4-04 owns Site FindingIndex/list, S4-02 owns money, and S6 owns Action/verification. The reusable cross-domain contract is stable Site-scoped episode ID, versioned matcher/transition, immutable revision-to-assessment/evidence lineage, recurrence/split/merge relationships, freshness/Unknown semantics and exact route selection; cold-chain excursion and EV session thresholds/fixtures wait for their domain slices.

S3-01 Q14â€“Q22 carries are ruled in the linked answer: retain only the proven capability-channel `INTENTIONAL_DELTA`, require comparable accepted evidence even for `MATCH`, and use the six-hour continuity bracket for comparison freshness. Keep today's UTC day as the honest default and the 60-row drill-down as an explicitly partial view; a bounded `S3-01u` usability carry will add assessed-day discovery and paged full evidence access without gating S4-01. Tests suffice for old-rule history; do not fabricate an obsolete-model walkthrough. The fixed fixture note and window-only FAILED/STALE result stand. Commit a stable fixture specification, keeping regenerated IDs in environment-bound run artifacts rather than presenting a stale per-run manifest as current. No further owner choice is requested; later owner review gates remain.

Decision: `D-2026-10-12-s4-finding-phase-1-clarifications`. Codex Architect rules on S4-01 Q1â€“Q9 in `.agent/architect-S4-01-answers-1.md`, subject to the independent phase-1 review. This amends `D-2026-10-12-finding-episode-and-revisions` where a more precise persistence rule is required. For a cross-window Finding, `UNIQUE(finding_id,assessment_revision_id)` is insufficient because another supporting assessment may change while the primary stays fixed. Keep the primary assessment FK and use unique `(finding_id,revision_no)` and `(finding_id,basis_sha256,transition)`, with an exact relation to every supporting interval/window assessment revision. The canonical basis includes selected window revision IDs as well as interval revision IDs, cited evidence, controls/bindings, claim bounds/limits and model/matcher versions; otherwise a Finding could silently continue to cite a no-longer-selected window basis. A changed selected control or window revision appends a FindingRevision even when candidate minutes stay equal. Call a provenance-only change `BASIS_REFRESHED`; reserve `SUPPORT_CHANGED` for a changed supported span, duration, claim or limitation.

The 20-minute episode bridge may cross an observed Necessary segment or an observed Indeterminate segment whose only limits are documented modelling uncertainty, provided every instant of the gap is covered by coherent observed segments and no evidence gap, contradiction or cohort ambiguity intervenes. Window coverage alone and a vacuously empty segment list cannot establish that bridge. A separated new Finding receives `recurs_after_finding_id` only when a public observed OFF establishes an actual break from the earlier generator episode; with an unobserved separation, open an unlinked Finding and show recurrence relation unknown. This narrows the earlier â€œevidenced separationâ€ wording; elapsed time alone is not evidence of an OFF.

S4-01's F01 baseline Candidate must be demonstrated through the ordinary registered simulated gateway, gateway-sealed Batch V2 and central acceptance; the already reviewed S3 normal-path case may be reused if its public IDs and selected S4 fixture database are verified. Later held/contradictory/bridge cases may use separately labelled authenticated Batch V2 fault fixtures, never described as gateway-sealed or as private Lab truth. Lifecycle remains append-only in FindingRevision/relations; `finding.closed_at` is unnecessary if reads derive closure at the selected revision. A fixture-only internal FoundationService publication of generator minimum-runtime is acceptable without adding an S4 product route, but must be labelled as a fixture control and keep exact revision lineage. Spec `decided` instants are ordering labels; product acceptance uses actual server decision times. Phase-2/3 reads must expose these provenance and lifecycle distinctions. No further owner choice is required; the S4-01 owner checkpoint remains.

Decision: `D-2026-10-12-s4-finding-phase-1-fix-clarifications`. Codex Architect resolves S4-01 Q10â€“Q15 in `.agent/architect-S4-01-answers-2.md`, subject to the fresh phase-1 fix re-review. A current Indeterminate reassessment overlapping a previously `NO_LONGER_SUPPORTED` episode may append `UNCONFIRMED`; it does not erase the earlier closed revision or assert renewed Candidate. A supported Candidate over the same episode without a proven separated break appends `REOPENED` to that stable Finding, not a recurrence. A â‰¤20-minute stop between candidate spans can bridge identity only when both bounding OFF reports are accepted, ordered and no contrary report intervenes; it does not add Candidate runtime through the stop. A new episode gets an immutable recurrence link only with full assessed separation and observed OFF after the prior run and before the new one. Include selected window revision/input basis in every Finding basis as already ruled, even when this yields many `BASIS_REFRESHED` revisions after same-day reassessment or backdated configuration. Keep each immutable revision and exact ID addressable; phase-2 read returns transition, prior/current selected assessment IDs, cause and unchanged-versus-changed support facts. Decision: `D-2026-10-12-s4-finding-phase-2-read-boundaries`. Codex Architect resolves S4-01 Q16â€“Q20 in `.agent/architect-S4-01-answers-3.md`, subject to the fresh phase-2 review. It must veto the bridge on a contradictory, non-GOOD, wrong-cohort or otherwise unaccounted intervening report; missing proof stays unbridged. They are listed separately in its report and activate by `D-2026-10-13-g-gap-packet-activation`: after the active V-01b closes and merges, G-01 takes the next free single-active slot, then G-02 takes the next free slot after reviewed and merged G-01 and merged/accepted V-01a, ahead of an ordinary ranked selection once any checker refusal or owner stop is resolved. G-03â€“G-05 wait for their reviewed merged UI and packet prerequisites; run at the first free slot with no ready active-wave core candidate, or take their remaining backlog to W1-exit review. A â‰¤5% ranking contest still goes to the owner before activating a due G packet; a 5â€“10% notice is recorded. This ordering needs no new owner choice and gives no G node a demo value. The screen ledger's `gap_backfill_task` is a planning pointer, not a delivered gap ID. G-01 introduces the catalogue schema and one real tagged Readiness gateway-firmware missing state; G-02 enrolls the four V-01a screens for complete non-core/open `gap_ids` coverage, then later batches enroll their merged UI.

## Screen-outward planning gate

The [v6.9 screen realization map](../frontend/docs/architecture/v69-screen-realization-map.md), [screen ledger](../frontend/docs/architecture/v69-screen-ledger.json), [model homes](../frontend/docs/architecture/v69-model-homes.json) and [panel contracts](../frontend/docs/architecture/v69-panel-contracts.json) are Architect planning overlays, not accepted screen completion. They account for 110 manifest IDs, 88 model names and 258 visible panels. The owner approved `Real with honest gaps` as the W1 target for 27 screens, including six moved from W4, and their exact core keys/values; later-wave tiers remain pending. The manifest ID and its `visual_reference` key each later comparison; `GAP` in a panel contract means no supported claim yet. Run `python frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_ledger.py` after ledger changes.

For each `Deferred` screen, the ledger's `promotion_dependencies.to_candidate` lists the design, projection, fixture and task gates before it can enter the executable queue. For every proposed `Real with honest gaps` screen, `promotion_dependencies.to_real` names unresolved panels and remaining work before full mock fidelity can be claimed. A named S task alone does not satisfy that gate. The Planner checks these fields when refining a packet and updates them when a dependency is met or the owner changes a tier.

S1 and S2 are merged; W1 owner input and `A-S3-dispatch` are recorded. **Before S3-01 is activated**, pass PRE-S3-03/startup guards and check the split [V-01 umbrella](V-01-v69-visual-fidelity.md). S3-01 contains the versioned `I-sim-dispatch-cases` fixture as phase 1, followed by typed assessment and UI/cutover phases; its Candidate path stays Indeterminate until the new capability channels are accepted. S3-02 owns the later positive Evidence Analysis claim-limit view. S4-03 owns Finding Evidence/History; S4-04 owns the Site Finding list. S5-01 owns selected-window context/timeline/money; S5-02 integrates the list with separate empty and positive fixtures. S6-01 owns Action detail/activity/change, S6-02 owns plan/evidence/outcome, and S6-03 owns the Site Work list. Resolve only model gaps merited by the next supported claim; broader work, domains and indexes stay in later waves. Unrefined S4-S7 packets remain outlines until their technical gates and owner checkpoints pass. A target tier does not authorize an empty route or illustrative mock value as product data.

S2-02 refinement compared `queue_baseline_nodes`: `I-accepted-evidence-store`, `I-edge-diagnostics-store` and `I-S2-02` remain in S2-02; S2-01b/c retain their nodes. None leaves the baseline. The registry and checker include 19 S1â€“S7 task packets, including S4-04, S5-02 and S6-03. Changed W1 core panel paths await independent graph/mapping review; unchanged reviewed paths keep their citations. Owner target approval is recorded separately.

## Non-executable follow-on dependency lane after S7

These are **planning lanes, not task IDs or executable packets**. Review the owner-approved tier and the ledger's exact panel contract before creating a slice. Keep multi-site and multi-window fixtures traceable through normal ingestion. A global comparison needs aligned windows, coverage, currency/price basis and non-overlap rules; Unknown stays visible when those conditions are absent.

| --- | --- | --- |

| Cold-chain and EV Sites | `cold`, `temperature`, `cooling`, `coldbackup`, `incident`, `workverification`, `cold-foundation`, `cold-health`, `cold-evidence`, `cold-evidence-analysis`, `cold-financials`, `cold-assumptions`, `cold-findings`, `cold-work`, `cold-work-incidents`, `cold-work-maintenance`, `cold-finding`, `cold-recommendation`, `cold-finding-excursion`, `cold-action`, `cold-scenarios`, `cold-readiness`, `cold-sld`, `wr-overview`, `wr-record`, `wr-history`; `ev`, `sessions`, `availability`, `energy`, `ev-foundation`, `ev-health`, `ev-evidence`, `ev-evidence-analysis`, `ev-financials`, `ev-assumptions`, `ev-findings`, `ev-work`, `ev-work-incidents`, `ev-work-maintenance`, `ev-finding`, `ev-recommendation`, `ev-action`, `ev-scenarios`, `ev-readiness`, `ev-sld` | Separate Site/role and captured-device fixtures, domain assessments, bounded Finding/consequence and work semantics through the shared ingestion path; missing sensors/sessions remain Unknown. |

Unsupported routes stay hidden or honestly unavailable; populated, missing and conflicting/late states come from persisted source-to-claim data, never copied mock values. ## Current-code transition by slice

| Slice | Keep/adapt | Replace or retire at the visible cutover |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | Existing Site shell/Foundation presentation and explicit Kobo fixture | YAML/JSON Site writes for the supported Foundation path become Postgres revisions; old files remain read-only comparison inputs. |
| S2-01a/b/c | Simulated observation transform, mapping and envelope publisher | Kobo Lab uses the registered snapshot; a separate SQLite-backed simulated gateway owns V3 identity, then diagnostics and fault scenarios. Physical protocol proof waits for the first real pilot. |
| S2-02 | Existing ingestion validation/deduplication and Evidence UI | Batch V1 remains readable for migration; authenticated V2 HTTPS receipts and Postgres decisions replace in-process Commit-to-ingest and JSON evidence as product authority. |
| S3-S5 | Existing dispatch, Finding and Site presentation where claim semantics still fit | Hard-coded device keys, JSON assessment authority and independent view recalculation give way to typed accepted-evidence inputs, revisioned claims and derived read models. |
| S6-S7 | Existing Lab control and Site shell where they fit | Persist Action/verification/health claims from public evidence and work records; no Lab truth or static mock value becomes product history. |

This is a cutover map, not permission to delete historical fixtures or completed task evidence. The technical design's demo-to-Postgres comparison must pass per supported read: Evidence changes in S2-02 after evidence comparison; Performance changes in S3-01 after dispatch comparison. S2-01b makes the gateway durable, S2-01c exposes diagnostics/faults, and S2-02 makes upload and central acceptance real; S3 consumes durable recompute jobs. Do not call the current in-process publisher or JSON ingestion the production gateway/ledger.

All packets inherit the current architecture and technical design. Every closeout needs the selected-window screen, read API, storage/reload, missing or contradictory evidence state, and lineage to the accepted SourceEnvelope. S2 additionally proves loss/retry/duplicate/conflict; S3â€“S4 preserve revisions after late evidence; S6 proves a failed guardrail. Follow `.ai/WORKFLOW.md` for independent review and appropriate checks. The four owner-review moments are S2 evidence, S4 bounded money, S6 verification and S7 Site Health. Global indexes, extra domains and empty tabs follow the Site journey.

.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md:7:The only authoritative authored-demonstration designation is a server-authorized SYSTEM publication committed **before** admission of its exact Batch V2. Pin tenant/Site UUID, gateway/source, external batch ID, canonical payload SHA-256, immutable publication ID and fixture/spec ID/version. Resolve service principal, authorization basis, channel, trigger and recorded time on the server. An identical registration retry returns the same ID; changed content or reuse of a publication ID fails. A gateway credential, client text, `SIMULATED`, Site/day match or missing designation proves no authorship.
.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md-8-
.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md-9-Registration and `open_batch` must take the same transaction-scoped lock on the scoped batch key and check for an existing batch while holding it. Registration after any batch admission fails, including a partially completed batch. `open_batch` binds at most the exact preauthorized commitment in its admission transaction; a matching-key but mismatched commitment fails rather than silently becoming unknown. Accepted IDs inherit that immutable binding, including after chunk restart. Duplicates retain their original accepted ID and provenance; rejected/conflict rows acquire none. Existing accepted rows remain unknown, with no retrospective spec/date backfill. The fixture command stays server-side with an explicit configured authority; no public designation route.

.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md-34-
.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md:35:The blanket `UNKNOWN` default is intentional for unbound historical rows and genuinely unknown or broken input, but not for every future ordinary simulated run. Separate source class from authorship. S4-05 must support two positive, immutable, server-authorized pre-admission batch bindings: `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` for an identified authored fixture/spec version (P01 and the authored P02 late-power fixture), and `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` for ordinary simulator output with a server-recorded run ID, immutable run identity/digest and exact tenant/Site, gateway/source, batch ID and payload digest. The latter requires a registered `SIMULATED` gateway and proof that this exact batch was produced by that recorded run; `source_mode=SIMULATED`, a gateway credential, client text, Site/day match or a private manifest alone does not grant the designation. The server may keep private scenario truth outside product evidence; the public binding carries only run identity and provenance needed for the read. Extend the same ledger publication/admission lock, append-only binding, duplicate and no-retroactive-backfill rules to this class. Do not reinterpret `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` as authored fixture or physical measurement.
.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md-36-
.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md-37-Resolve each selected FindingRevision's cited accepted IDs and each consequence revision's union with its own power-interval IDs. All IDs bound to one coherent authored fixture ID/version yield `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION`; all IDs bound to a coherent registered simulation run yield `REGISTERED_SIMULATION`. Multiple run IDs require an explicitly recorded compatible run basis; absent that, return `MIXED`. Crossing source classes, mixing bound and unbound IDs, or conflicting fixture/version/run bases is `MIXED`. No positive binding, an empty citation set, or any missing, foreign-Site or broken link is `UNKNOWN`; a broken link takes precedence. Return exact IDs and reasons. An authored or registered-simulation set may show a current amount only after the existing LR, exact-head, currentness, source-open, quantity and price gates pass, with a truthful `Demonstration data` or `Simulated data` label respectively. `MIXED`/`UNKNOWN` show no current amount and historical values stay audit-only. Existing unbound ordinary simulated and real rows therefore remain `UNKNOWN` with no current amount; new ordinary runs need the positive binding. A real gateway, even with `source_mode=LIVE`, has no positive source designation from S4-05 and cannot show current money yet.
.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md-38-

S6-01 creates one immutable, tenant/Site-scoped Action identity from a reviewed persisted `FindingRevision` under the canonical Finding route. It pins the originating revision, selected assessment and accepted-evidence/configuration basis; a later Finding revision does not silently rewrite that origin. A Finding may have several Actions, but each accepted recommendation creates or idempotently returns one Action for its server-defined command key. The recommendation is a bounded proposed reserve-SOC policy trial with explicit purpose, limitation and source Finding; it is not a measured saving or verified outcome. The Action detail root is `/sites/{site}/actions/{action_id}` with stable child routes and exact `event_id` selection, and Site/Finding back-links. Action state is derived from an append-only, server-ordered `ActionEvent` ledger. Record stable event ID, Action and Site, event type, `occurred_at`, server `recorded_at`, idempotency key, actor kind/ID, source channel, authorization basis and triggering command/job or content revision. Idempotent identical retries return the event; conflicting reuse and cross-Site references fail. Historical reads select a recorded cutoff and then `(recorded_at,id)` order; a backdated `occurred_at` is displayed as such but never rewrites the audit order. `PROPOSED`/`ACCEPTED`, application `PENDING`/`APPLIED`/`FAILED`, and later verification status are separate dimensions. Acceptance does not assert application; an applied change or completed work does not assert benefit. A failed application preserves its attempt, error and retry relation without an effective-policy claim. No human author, approval or attestation is inferred from a gateway credential, client field, local OS user or SYSTEM fixture. Under the existing `A-actor-identity` rule, human writes need a verified operator principal; otherwise only a clearly labelled, server-authorized SYSTEM demonstration command with stable service/job provenance is allowed, or the write stays unavailable.

Validate the expected old revision and Site scope under the publish lock; reject stale, unauthorized or invalid edits and record a failed attempt without claiming the new setting took effect. `APPLIED` requires confirmation of the persisted new effective revision; an ActionEvent alone is insufficient. The old basis and every event/change revision remain addressable after reload. Do not mutate a Lab-only override or rewrite accepted evidence. 5-## Q1 - authoritative publication moment: accept with transaction detail
6-
7:The only authoritative authored-demonstration designation is a server-authorized SYSTEM publication committed **before** admission of its exact Batch V2. Pin tenant/Site UUID, gateway/source, external batch ID, canonical payload SHA-256, immutable publication ID and fixture/spec ID/version. Resolve service principal, authorization basis, channel, trigger and recorded time on the server. An identical registration retry returns the same ID; changed content or reuse of a publication ID fails. A gateway credential, client text, `SIMULATED`, Site/day match or missing designation proves no authorship.
8-
9-Registration and `open_batch` must take the same transaction-scoped lock on the scoped batch key and check for an existing batch while holding it. Registration after any batch admission fails, including a partially completed batch. `open_batch` binds at most the exact preauthorized commitment in its admission transaction; a matching-key but mismatched commitment fails rather than silently becoming unknown. Accepted IDs inherit that immutable binding, including after chunk restart. Duplicates retain their original accepted ID and provenance; rejected/conflict rows acquire none. Existing accepted rows remain unknown, with no retrospective spec/date backfill. The fixture command stays server-side with an explicit configured authority; no public designation route.

An empty citation set, no designated IDs, or any missing, foreign-Site or broken accepted-ID/publication link is `UNKNOWN`; a broken link takes precedence over `MIXED`, and the read names the problematic IDs. A server-authorized, state-neutral SYSTEM demonstration `OBSERVED` event with fixed `DEMO_REVIEW_NOTE` code may carry an explicit `occurred_at` between ACCEPTED and server `recorded_at`, no more than 30 days earlier, to prove backdated history without changing application state. SYSTEM demonstration commands require a server-issued scoped capability and explicit label. The full M1-M3 ruling is in `.agent/architect-S6-01-answers.md` under "Follow-up (plan review M1-M3)". S6-01a/S6-01b packets must incorporate the named changes before implementation; S6-01b still waits for the owner's actual visual walkthrough before merge.

Finding and consequence source state derives from each revision's exact accepted IDs, including consequence power intervals. +# Architect ruling - S5-01 phase-1 review M3 (2026-10-15)
+
+Authority: `.agent/architect-S5-01-M3-request.md`, the independent S5-01 phase-1 review M3, `tasks/LR-01-late-reading-series-and-money-limit.md`, `tasks/S5-01-site-overview.md`, the P02 late-power spec, the owner's S4-02b demonstration ruling, and `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`.
+
+## Decision
+
+Choose (a): add `backend/conversions/s4-power-late-demo-cases-v1.json` to the shared `demonstration_case` declaration now. For KOBO-001, the P02-power-late case on 2026-10-24 must return the same visible `Demonstration data` label and statement used for authored P01, with P02's case ID and spec label. Apply the existing bounded UTC episode/day matching rule. An unreadable or malformed declared spec must retain the cautious unverified result and block a current amount; an absent case match alone is not a positive source attestation. Refuse conflicting declarations rather than silently picking one. This is a temporary fixture-based presentation rule, not stored evidence authorship or a general real-source classification.
+
+Put the shared-label change and its P02 regression proof on **LR-01**. LR-01 introduced the late fixture's current-money Financials path, so its eligible Finding/Financials walkthrough should already show P02's authored label beside the exact 15.8125 USD amount (15.82 USD displayed) before its owner checkpoint. S5-01 consumes the same helper and should verify its P02 Overview money card shows that label after `attest` and settled `tick`, while withheld, pending, stale and closed states still show no current amount. Recheck the shared Finding/Financials and Overview APIs as well as the visible cards after reload. The LR-01 and S5-01 owner walkthroughs remain held for the owner's actual visual check at about 22:00 EAT; this ruling is not that acceptance.
+
+Do not turn `ELIGIBLE` or a matching day into amount authority. Current money still requires the exact-head `CURRENT PRICED` consequence, current source, quantity/price and all existing read gates. When S4-05 lands, replace the day/spec heuristic with its stored exact-revision provenance for both P01 and P02. Unbound or mixed cited evidence then follows `MIXED_OR_UNKNOWN_SOURCE` with no current amount, including old fixture rows; no retrospective date-based authorship is permitted.

A server-authorized, state-neutral SYSTEM demonstration `OBSERVED` event with fixed `DEMO_REVIEW_NOTE` code may carry an explicit `occurred_at` between ACCEPTED and server `recorded_at`, no more than 30 days earlier, to prove backdated history without changing application state. SYSTEM demonstration commands require a server-issued scoped capability and explicit label. The full M1-M3 ruling is in `.agent/architect-S6-01-answers.md` under "Follow-up (plan review M1-M3)". S6-01a/S6-01b packets must incorporate the named changes before implementation; S6-01b still waits for the owner's actual visual walkthrough before merge.
 
-Decision: `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`. Under the owner's 2026-10-15 absence delegation, accept S4-05 Q1-Q3 as amended by Follow-up (plan review M1) in `.agent/architect-S4-05-answers.md`. A server-authorized, immutable pre-admission publication binds an exact Batch V2 commitment to tenant/Site, gateway/source, external batch ID and canonical digest. `AUTHORED_DEMONSTRATION` additionally binds fixture/spec ID and version, including authored P01 and P02. `REGISTERED_SIMULATION` binds an ordinary simulator batch to a server-recorded run identity; `SIMULATED` mode or a private manifest alone is insufficient. Publication and admission serialize on the scoped batch key; post-admission registration and mismatched commitments fail. Accepted IDs inherit immutable provenance, duplicates retain their original accepted ID, and historical unbound IDs stay unknown. Finding and consequence source state derives from each revision's exact accepted IDs, including consequence power intervals. Coherent all-authored or all-registered-simulation sets may show a current amount only when LR eligibility, exact basis, currentness, source-open and quantity/price gates also pass. Mixed or unknown source blocks a current amount in Finding Financials, consequence and S5 Overview regardless of `CURRENT PRICED` or LR eligibility; prior values remain audit-only. Real data remains unknown and unable to show current money until a separate server-authorized `ATTESTED_REAL_SOURCE` follow-on is implemented. Create open registry node `I-attested-real-source-provenance` in `real_pilot_requires` before resolving `I-stored-demo-provenance` or installing a pilot timer; S4-05 may resolve before the real-source node, but its resolution is not pilot readiness. Amend S5-01's source gate to consume stored exact-revision provenance whichever slice merges second. Extend the existing PostgreSQL evidence-ledger writer, not the writer count: rule 8 permits only its INSERTs to publication/link rows, refuses other writers and mutations, preserves existing writer protections, and is backed by database immutability. Visible copy and pilot designation still require the owner's W1 checkpoint; this decision grants neither owner approval nor W1/pilot readiness.

+# Architect ruling - S5-01 phase-1 review M3 (2026-10-15)
+
+Authority: `.agent/architect-S5-01-M3-request.md`, the independent S5-01 phase-1 review M3, `tasks/LR-01-late-reading-series-and-money-limit.md`, `tasks/S5-01-site-overview.md`, the P02 late-power spec, the owner's S4-02b demonstration ruling, and `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`.
+
+## Decision
+
+Choose (a): add `backend/conversions/s4-power-late-demo-cases-v1.json` to the shared `demonstration_case` declaration now. For KOBO-001, the P02-power-late case on 2026-10-24 must return the same visible `Demonstration data` label and statement used for authored P01, with P02's case ID and spec label. Apply the existing bounded UTC episode/day matching rule. An unreadable or malformed declared spec must retain the cautious unverified result and block a current amount; an absent case match alone is not a positive source attestation. Refuse conflicting declarations rather than silently picking one. This is a temporary fixture-based presentation rule, not stored evidence authorship or a general real-source classification.
+
+Put the shared-label change and its P02 regression proof on **LR-01**. LR-01 introduced the late fixture's current-money Financials path, so its eligible Finding/Financials walkthrough should already show P02's authored label beside the exact 15.8125 USD amount (15.82 USD displayed) before its owner checkpoint. S5-01 consumes the same helper and should verify its P02 Overview money card shows that label after `attest` and settled `tick`, while withheld, pending, stale and closed states still show no current amount. Recheck the shared Finding/Financials and Overview APIs as well as the visible cards after reload. The LR-01 and S5-01 owner walkthroughs remain held for the owner's actual visual check at about 22:00 EAT; this ruling is not that acceptance.
+
+Do not turn `ELIGIBLE` or a matching day into amount authority. Current money still requires the exact-head `CURRENT PRICED` consequence, current source, quantity/price and all existing read gates. When S4-05 lands, replace the day/spec heuristic with its stored exact-revision provenance for both P01 and P02. Unbound or mixed cited evidence then follows `MIXED_OR_UNKNOWN_SOURCE` with no current amount, including old fixture rows; no retrospective date-based authorship is permitted.
